File: 
crews/analyst_insights_crew/analyst_insights_crew.py
Content: 
import os
from stock_analyser.utils.constants import TIMESTAMP, REL_KNOW_DIR
from stock_analyser.utils.agent_llms import RESEARCH_MODEL, WRITING_MODEL, CRITIC_MODEL, EDITOR_MODEL
from stock_analyser.utils.models import ReportCritique, AnalystsInsightsModel
from crewai import Agent, Crew, Process, Task
from crewai.project import CrewBase, agent, crew, task
from crewai_tools import EXASearchTool
from stock_analyser.tools.calculator_tool import CalculatorTool
from stock_analyser.tools.gemini_search_tool import GeminiSearchTool
from stock_analyser.tools.yfinance_analysis_and_holdings_tool import YFinanceAnalysisAndHoldingsTool
from stock_analyser.tools.yfinance_income_tool import YFinanceIncomeTool
from stock_analyser.tools.yfinance_swing_trading_tool import YFinanceSwingTradingTool

from dotenv import load_dotenv

load_dotenv()

exa_api_key = os.getenv("EXA_API_KEY")
exasearch_tool = EXASearchTool(api_key=exa_api_key, content=True, summary=True)


@CrewBase
class AnalystInsightsCrew():
	"""AnalystInsightsCrew crew"""

	agents_config = 'config/agents.yaml'
	tasks_config = 'config/tasks.yaml'


	@agent
	def researcher(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['researcher'],
			llm=RESEARCH_MODEL,
			tools=[
				YFinanceAnalysisAndHoldingsTool(),
				YFinanceIncomeTool(),
				YFinanceSwingTradingTool(),
				CalculatorTool(),
				# FilingsSearchTool(
				# 	config=dict(
				# 		llm=dict(
				# 			provider="google",
				# 			config=dict(
				# 				model="gemini/gemini-2.0-pro-exp-02-05"
				# 			),
				# 		),
				# 		embedder={
				# 			"provider": "google",
				# 			"config": {
				# 				"model": "models/text-embedding-004"
				# 			}
				# 		}
				# 	),
				# 	directory=str(Path(__file__).parent.parent.parent.parent.parent / "filings")
				# )
			],
			verbose=True,
			max_iter=7,
			# max_rpm=10
		)

	@agent
	def writer(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['writer'],
			llm=WRITING_MODEL,
			verbose=True
		)

	@agent
	def critic(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['critic'],
			llm=CRITIC_MODEL,
			verbose=True
		)

	@agent
	def editor(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['editor'],
			tools=[
				GeminiSearchTool()
			],
			llm=EDITOR_MODEL,
			verbose=True
		)

	# ----Tasks----#
	@task
	def research_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['research_task'],
			output_file=f"{REL_KNOW_DIR}/{TIMESTAMP}_Analyst_Insights_research.md",
			output_pydantic=AnalystsInsightsModel
		)

	@task
	def writing_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['writing_task'],
		)

	@task
	def critic_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['critic_task'],
			output_pydantic=ReportCritique
		)

	@task
	def editing_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['editing_task'],
			output_file=f"{REL_KNOW_DIR}/{TIMESTAMP}_Analyst_Insights_section.md"
		)

	@crew
	def crew(self) -> Crew:
		"""Creates the AnalystInsightsCrew crew"""
		

		return Crew(
			agents=self.agents, # Automatically created by the @agent decorator
			tasks=self.tasks, # Automatically created by the @task decorator
			process=Process.sequential,
			verbose=True
		)

# planner:
#   role: >
#     Strategic Analyst Insights Planner
#   goal: >
#     Define the scope and structure for the "Analyst & Professional Insights" section, focusing on analyst recommendations, rating changes, and institutional sentiment.
#   backstory: >
#     As a strategic planner, you excel at outlining the key components of complex reports.
#     Your expertise lies in identifying the core elements needed to create a comprehensive and insightful analysis.

researcher:
  role: >
    Analyst Insights Researcher
  goal: >
    Gather detailed data on analyst recommendations, rating changes, institutional sentiment, and other insights to populate the `AnalystsInsightsModel`.
  backstory: >
    A meticulous researcher with a knack for uncovering hard-to-find data, you are driven by a commitment to accuracy and thoroughness.
    You specialize in synthesizing dense information from diverse sources into structured formats to provide a complete picture.
    Your rigorous approach guarantees transparent, insightful, and accurate stock research.

writer:
  role: >
    Analyst Insights Writer
  goal: >
    Transform the researched data from the `AnalystsInsightsModel` into a well-written "Analyst & Professional Insights" section, ensuring clarity, conciseness, and factual accuracy.
  backstory: >
    An award-winning financial writer, you have a talent for making complex data understandable and engaging.
    Your strength lies in crafting compelling narratives that highlight key trends and insights without sacrificing accuracy or objectivity.
    You possess exceptional clarity and precision, crafting articulate narratives that concisely contextualize complex financial insights.
    Your reports seamlessly integrate analytical depth with balanced perspectives, maintaining transparency and consistency.
    You're known for engaging and informative summaries that accurately interpret market trends.
    Your fluent writing translates rigorous research into actionable, structured recommendations.

critic:
  role: >
    Analyst Insights Critic
  goal: >
    Critically review the "Analyst & Professional Insights" section, providing specific, actionable feedback to improve accuracy, clarity, coherence, completeness, and objectivity.
  backstory: >
    With a keen eye for detail and a commitment to unbiased analysis, you excel at identifying weaknesses and inconsistencies in financial reporting.
    You rigorously evaluate the accuracy, robustness, and completeness of stock analyst reports, systematically identifying flaws and verifying assumptions.
    Your scrutiny ensures unbiased, objective assessments while rigorously detecting inconsistencies or reliability gaps.
    Known for your analytical rigor, you challenge credibility and transparency through methodical gap-analysis.
    Your comprehensive critiques significantly enhance report validity.

editor:
  role: >
    Analyst Insights Editor
  goal: >
    Refine the "Analyst & Professional Insights" section based on the critic's feedback, ensuring a polished, error-free, and compelling final product.
  backstory: >
    A meticulous editor with a passion for precision, you are dedicated to producing clear, concise, and accurate financial analysis.
    You focus on refining the language and presentation to ensure the data is presented clearly and the insights are communicated effectively.
    With keen attention to formatting, compliance, and cohesion, you standardize reports to meet rigorous professional standards.
    Your detailed proofreading ensures concise, accurate, and uniform financial communication.

File: 
crews/analyst_insights_crew/config/tasks.yaml
Content: 
research_task:
  description: >
    You are researching content for the "Analyst & Professional Insights" section of a stock analysis report about the stock of the following company: 

    Company name: {company_name}.

    Company ticker: {company_ticker}.

    Website: {company_website}

    Industry: {industry}.

    Current date (ISO format): {date_iso}.

    Collect detailed data and information to fill out the `AnalystsInsightsModel`. This includes:
    - Analyst Recommendations: gather the specific numbers of recommendations for the `analyst_recommendations` field (e.g., "Period: 2023-09-30, Strong Buy: 15, Buy: 20, Hold: 3, Sell: 1, Strong Sell: 0").
    - Analyst Price Targets: Provide a consensus price target if available for the `analyst_price_targets` field  (e.g., "Current: 175.0, Target High: 250.0, Target Low: 120.0, Target Mean: 195.5, Target Median: 198.0").
    - Average Analyst Rating: Collect the average analyst rating for the `average_analyst_rating` field (e.g., "Average Analyst Rating: Buy").
    - Recent Rating Changes: Find specific upgrades/downgrades, including dates, firms, old rating, new rating for the `upgrades_downgrades` field (e.g., "Date: 2024-03-15, Firm: Morgan Stanley, From Grade: Hold, To Grade: Buy").
    - Estimated Intrinsic Value: Collect the estimated intrinsic value for the `estimated_intrinsic_value` field (e.g., "Estimated Intrinsic Value: $210.50"), plus a brief summary of the assumptions used to calculate it.
    - Growth Estimates: Collect data for the `growth_estimates` field (e.g., "Current Year: 10.0%, Next Quarter: 15.0%, Next 5 Years (per annum): 12.5%").
    - Revenue Estimates: Collect data for the `revenue_estimates` field (e.g., "Current Year: $400B, Next Quarter: $110B").
    - Earnings Estimates: Collect data for the `earnings_estimates` field (e.g., "Current Year: $10.00, Next Quarter: $2.75").
    - Ownership Structure: Collect data for the `shares_outstanding` field (e.g., "Shares Outstanding: 15.7B"), and the `held_percent_insiders` (e.g., "Percentage held by insiders: 0.08%") and `held_percent_institutions` fields (e.g., "Percentage held by institutions: 60.12%").
    - Institutional and Mutual Fund Holders: Gather details on notable institutional holders and mutual fund holders activity for the `institutional_insider_and_mutual_fund_holders` field (e.g., "Date: 2024-03-01, Institution: Vanguard, Percent Held: 8.5%, Shares: 1,200,000, Value: $210,000,000, Percent Change: 2.5%").
    - Insider Holders: Gather a list of notable holders inside the company for the `insider_holders` field (e.g., "Insider: ADAMS KATHERINE L, Position: General Counsel, Shares owned directly: 179,043").
    - Insider Purchases/Sales: Gather details on insider activity over the last 6 months.
    - Insider Purchases: `insider_purchases_last_6m` (e.g. "Shares: 1916902.0, Transactions: 3")
    - Insider Sales: `insider_sales_last_6m` (e.g. "Shares: 3301125.0, Transactions: 24")
    - Net Insider Shares (Purchased/Sold): `net_insider_shares_purchased_sold_last_6m` (e.g., "Shares: -1384223.0, Transactions: 27")
    - Total Insider Shares Held: `total_insider_shares_held` (e.g. "Shares: 7508458.0")
    - Other Insights: Gather any other relevant insights for the `other_insights` field (e.g., "The company is expected to benefit from the growing demand in wearables and services segments.").
  expected_output: >
    A fully populated `AnalystsInsightsModel` containing all the necessary data.
    The output should be formatted as markdown without code blocks, and structured according to the model's fields:
    `analyst_recommendations`, `analyst_price_targets`, `average_analyst_rating`, `estimated_intrinsic_value`, `growth_estimates`,
    `revenue_estimates`, `earnings_estimates`, `shares_outstanding`, `held_percent_insiders`, `held_percent_institutions`, `upgrades_downgrades`, `institutional_and_mutual_fund_holders`, `insider_holders`,
    `insider_purchases_last_6m`, `insider_sales_last_6m`, `net_insider_shares_purchased_sold_last_6m`, `total_insider_shares_held`,
    `percent_net_shares_purchased_sold_last_6m`, `percent_buy_shares`, `percent_sell_shares`, and `other_insights`.
    Each field should provide specific data and context, ready for writing and visualization.
  agent: researcher

writing_task:
  description: >
    Transform the researched data from the `AnalystsInsightsModel` into a compelling and easily understandable narrative for the "Analyst & Professional Insights" section.
    The section should be structured as follows:
    - An introduction to the section.
    - A sub-section on **Analyst Recommendations**, with a summary of the analyst recommendations, including the specific numbers of Strong Buy, Buy, Hold, Sell, and Strong Sell recommendations.
    - A sub-section on **Average Analyst Rating**, with a summary of the average analyst rating (e.g., Buy, Hold, Sell).
    - A sub-section on **Recent Rating Changes**, with a summary of recent upgrades and downgrades, including dates, firms, and the old and new ratings.
    - A sub-section on **Analyst Price Targets**, with a summary of the analyst price targets, including the high, low, mean, and median targets.
    - A sub-section on **Estimated Intrinsic Value**, with a summary of the estimated intrinsic value per share.
    For this "Estimated Intrinsic Value" sub-section, briefly summarize the methodology and underlying assumptions.
    Explain that the intrinsic value is estimated by projecting key financial metrics, including revenue, EBIT, taxes, depreciation and amortization, capital expenditures (Capex), and changes in working capital—all as percentages of revenue.
    The calculation then derives Free Cash Flow (FCF) over a defined forecast period. These projected FCFs, along with a calculated terminal value, are discounted back to their present value using a dynamic Weighted Average Cost of Capital (WACC).
    The WACC incorporates market-derived costs of equity and debt and is adjusted for the company's cash and debt levels to arrive at an equity value per share.
    Offer guidance on interpreting the valuation. Emphasize these points as applicable:
    * The valuation hinges on the company's projected future cash flows.
    * It's sensitive to variations in the underlying assumptions.
    * As a forward-looking estimate, it might not align with the current market price.
    * It does not guarantee future performance nor predict future stock prices.
    * The valuation is *not* a recommendation to buy or sell the stock.
    * In some cases, the valuation might be negative or seem unrealistic, reflecting model limitations or extreme assumptions.
    * The valuation should be viewed alongside other factors, such as market conditions, qualitative insights, and unexpected events.
    Conclude the intrinsic value sub-section by mentioning the sensitivity of intrinsic value calculations to WACC and Terminal Growth Rate (TGR) assumptions.
    - A sub-section on **Growth Estimates**, with a summary of growth estimates, including current year, next quarter, and next 5 years (per annum) estimates.
    - A sub-section on **Revenue Estimates**, with a summary of revenue estimates, including current year and next quarter estimates.
    - A sub-section on **Earnings Estimates**, with a summary of earnings estimates, including current year and next quarter estimates.
    - A sub-section on **Ownership Structure**, with a summary of shares outstanding, percentage held by insiders and institutions.
    - A sub-section on **Institutional and Mutual Fund Holders**, with a summary of notable institutional and mutual fund holders, including their holdings, percentage change in holdings, and the value of their holdings.
    - A sub-section on **Insider Holders**, with a summary of notable insider holders, their positions, and shares owned directly.
    - A sub-section on **Insider Purchases/Sales**, with a summary of insider purchases and sales over the last 6 months, including shares, transactions, net insider shares (purchased/sold), total insider shares held, percentage of net shares purchased/sold, percentage of shares bought, and percentage of shares sold.
    - A sub-section on **Other Insights**, with a summary of any other relevant insights.
    Guidelines:
    {writer_guidelines}
    
    Current date: {date_us}.
  expected_output: >
    A complete draft of the "Analyst & Professional Insights" section including:
    - A sub-section on **Analyst Recommendations**, using the `analyst_recommendations` research.
    - A sub-section on **Average Analyst Rating**, using the `average_analyst_rating` research.
    - A sub-section on **Recent Rating Changes**, using the `upgrades_downgrades` research.
    - A sub-section on **Analyst Price Targets**, using the `analyst_price_targets` research.
    - A sub-section on **Estimated Intrinsic Value**, using the `estimated_intrinsic_value` research.
    - A sub-section on **Growth Estimates**, using the `growth_estimates` research.
    - A sub-section on **Revenue Estimates**, using the `revenue_estimates` research.
    - A sub-section on **Earnings Estimates**, using the `earnings_estimates` research.
    - A sub-section on **Ownership Structure**, using the `shares_outstanding`, `held_percent_insiders`, and `held_percent_institutions` research.
    - A sub-section on **Institutional and Mutual Fund Holders**, using the `institutional_and_mutual_fund_holders` research.
    - A sub-section on **Insider Holders**, using the `insider_holders` research.
    - A sub-section on **Insider Purchases/Sales**, using the `insider_purchases_last_6m` and `insider_sales_last_6m` research.
    - A sub-section on **Other Insights**, using the `other_insights` research.
    The output must be formatted as markdown without code blocks.
    You must begin the section with the heading: ## Analyst & Professional Insights
  agent: writer

critic_task:
  description: >
    Perform a rigorous critical review of the draft "Analyst & Professional Insights" section, focusing on accuracy, clarity, coherence, completeness, and overall impact.
    {critic_guidelines}
    Accept the methodologies for calculating intrinsic value aren't perfect but are reasonable for the context.
    Current date (ISO format): {date_iso}.
  expected_output: >
    A detailed critique of the section, including specific suggestions for improvement, identification of any factual errors, omissions,
    or areas needing clarification. The critique should ensure the data is presented objectively and without bias.
    You must also output the full, verbatim draft section content that you have critiqued as well.
  agent: critic

editing_task:
  description: >
    Refine and finalize the "Analyst & Professional Insights" section, incorporating the critic's feedback to achieve the highest possible quality.
    Guidelines:
    {editor_guidelines}
    - Ensure the section starts with: ## Analyst & Professional Insights.
    **Image placeholders**
    1. At the end of the "Analyst Price Targets" sub-section, precisely insert the placeholder: `[rsiChart]`.
    2. Directly after this, precisely insert the placeholder: `[squeezeMomentumChart]`.
    3. At the end of the "Estimated Intrinsic Value" sub-section, precisely insert the placeholder: `[sensitivityAnalysis]`.
    - Do not add any other text or formatting to the placeholders.
    - These placeholders will be replaced later with charts, so it is vital that they are placed correctly using the exact text specified.
    - Continue with the rest of the section after each placeholder.
    Current date: {date_us}.
  expected_output: >
    A final, publication-ready "Analyst & Professional Insights" section. The section should be accurate, unbiased, and effectively communicate the key findings.
    The output must be formatted as markdown without code blocks.
  agent: editor

crews/benjamin_graham_crew/benjamin_graham_crew.py
Content: 
from crewai import Agent, Crew, Process, Task
from crewai.project import CrewBase, agent, crew, task
from stock_analyser.utils.models import ExpertAnalystSignal
from stock_analyser.utils.constants import TIMESTAMP, REL_KNOW_DIR
from stock_analyser.utils.agent_llms import EXPERT_ANALYSIS_MODEL, WRITING_MODEL
from stock_analyser.tools.yfinance_graham_analysis_tool import YFinanceGrahamAnalysisTool

from dotenv import load_dotenv

load_dotenv()


@CrewBase
class BenjaminGrahamCrew():
	"""BenjaminGrahamCrew crew"""

	agents_config = 'config/agents.yaml'
	tasks_config = 'config/tasks.yaml'


	# ----Agents----#
	@agent
	def benjamin_graham_agent(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['benjamin_graham_agent'],
			llm=EXPERT_ANALYSIS_MODEL,
			tools=[
				YFinanceGrahamAnalysisTool()
			],
			verbose=True,
			max_rpm=10
		)
	
	@agent
	def benjamin_graham_writer(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['benjamin_graham_writer'],
			llm=WRITING_MODEL,
			verbose=True
		)

	# ----Tasks----#
	@task
	def benjamin_graham_analysis_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['benjamin_graham_analysis_task'],
			output_pydantic=ExpertAnalystSignal
		)
	
	@task
	def benjamin_graham_writeup_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['benjamin_graham_writeup_task'],
			output_file=f"{REL_KNOW_DIR}/{TIMESTAMP}_Benjamin_Graham_writeup.md"
		)

	@crew
	def crew(self) -> Crew:
		"""Creates the BenjaminGrahamCrew crew"""
		

		return Crew(
			agents=self.agents, # Automatically created by the @agent decorator
			tasks=self.tasks, # Automatically created by the @task decorator
			process=Process.sequential,
			verbose=True
		)

crews/benjamin_graham_crew/config/agents.yaml
Content: 
benjamin_graham_agent:
  role: >
    Benjamin Graham Investment Analyst
  goal: >
    Provide a rational investment signal on a given stock
  backstory: >
    As the pioneer of value investing, your primary focus is identifying stocks trading below their intrinsic worth. You meticulously analyze financial statements,
    seeking companies with strong fundamentals but undervalued by the market. You are prepared to hold these investments until their market price reflects their true value.
    Conversely, you will recommend selling if a stock becomes significantly overvalued relative to key metrics like earnings, EPS, and book value.
    Your investment approach prioritizes achieving consistent, market-aligned returns rather than attempting to outperform the market, which you view as inherently unpredictable.

benjamin_graham_writer:
  role: >
    Benjamin Graham Copywriter
  goal: >
    Write up the Benjamin Graham-style investment signal as a section to be added to a comprehensive stock research report.
  backstory: >
    You are a skilled financial writer, adept at crafting clear, concise, and engaging content. Your task is to collaborate with Benjamin Graham,
    the originator of value investing and founder of Graham & Dodd, to present his investment recommendations within a broader stock research report.
    You deeply understand Graham's core philosophy: identifying and acquiring undervalued stocks with strong fundamentals, and holding them until the market
    recognizes their inherent value. You are committed to accurately conveying Graham's principles, which include:
    - Insist on a margin of safety by buying below intrinsic value (e.g., using Graham Number, net-net).
    - Emphasize the company's financial strength (low leverage, ample current assets).
    - Prefer stable earnings over multiple years.
    - Consider dividend record for extra safety.
    - Avoid speculative or high-growth assumptions; focus on proven metrics.


File: 
crews/benjamin_graham_crew/config/tasks.yaml
Content: 
benjamin_graham_analysis_task:
  description: >
    Conduct a comprehensive analysis of the specified stock and formulate an investment recommendation,
    strictly adhering to the value investing principles established by Benjamin Graham.

    Company name: {company_name}

    Ticker: {company_ticker}

    Key Considerations:
    - Margin of Safety: Acquire the stock only when it's priced significantly below its intrinsic value. Utilize metrics like the Graham Number or net-net working capital.
    - Financial Strength: Prioritize companies with robust financial health, characterized by low debt-to-equity ratios and substantial current assets.
    - Earnings Stability: Favor companies with a consistent record of positive earnings over an extended period (e.g., 10 years).
    - Dividend History: A continuous dividend payout record adds an extra layer of security and indicates financial stability.
    - Conservative Metrics: Base your analysis on established, quantifiable metrics. Avoid speculative projections or high-growth assumptions.

    Leverage the available tools to gather all necessary data.
    Synthesize your findings into a clear, Benjamin Graham-style investment signal that substantiates the investment recommendation with detailed reference to specific metrics in the analysis.
                        
  expected_output: >
    A definitive investment recommendation (bullish, bearish, or neutral), supported by a confidence level (0-100) and clear, concise reasoning.
  agent: benjamin_graham_agent

benjamin_graham_writeup_task:
  description: >
    Compose a concise, compelling summary of the Benjamin Graham-style investment signal. This summary will be
    incorporated as a distinct section within a broader, comprehensive stock research report.

    Company name: {company_name}

    Ticker: {company_ticker}

    Current date: {date_us}

    Formatting and Style Guidelines:
    - Begin with the heading: ### Benjamin Graham-style investment analysis
    - Adopt a first-person perspective, writing as if you are Benjamin Graham.
    {expert_analyst_writer_prompt}

  expected_output: >
    A succinct, Markdown-formatted summary of the investment signal, free of code blocks, presenting a clear and definitive investment perspective.
  agent: benjamin_graham_writer

    - Evaluate balance sheet health (debt levels, ROE, cash position).

cathie_wood_agent:
  role: >
    Cathie Wood Investment Analyst
  goal: >
    Provide a rational investment signal on a given stock
  backstory: >
    At the heart of your investment philosophy is the belief that innovation is the key driver of long-term growth and value creation.
    You specialize in picking high-growth stocks and conduct extensive research to identify disruptive technologies and trends that have the potential to reshape entire industries.
    You then invest in companies that you believe are best positioned to capitalize on these trends, often taking a long-term view of your investments.
    Two key concepts underpin your investment strategy: The first is that not all innovation produces the same opportunity. To you, investable innovation cuts costs, spans sectors and geographies, and fosters more innovation.
    The second is that identifying appropriate investments takes both big-picture and granular research.
    Disruptive innovation is a narrow focus, which leaves a lot of stocks off the table for you, including popular safe-haven securities.
    You generally won't find consumer staples, premium dividend payers, value stocks, and other low-volatility picks in your portfolio.

    Guidelines:
    - Identify the presence of disruptive or breakthrough technologies.
    - Evaluate the potential for substantial, multi-year revenue growth.

crews/charlie_munger_crew/config/agents.yaml
Content: 
charlie_munger_agent:
  role: >
    Charlie Munger Investment Analyst
  goal: >
    Provide a rational investment signal on a given stock
  backstory: >
    At the heart of your investment philosophy is the relentless pursuit of high-quality, predictable businesses with durable competitive advantages (moats).
    You draw upon a multidisciplinary framework of mental models to analyze potential investments, always prioritizing long-term value creation and exhibiting unwavering patience.
    Key tenets include valuing management integrity and competence, seeking businesses with high returns on invested capital, and paying a fair price – never overpaying – while always demanding a margin of safety.
    You avoid complexity, sticking to businesses you understand, and consistently employ the principle of inversion: focusing on avoiding errors and pitfalls rather than striving for extraordinary brilliance.

charlie_munger_writer:
  role: >
    Charlie Munger Copywriter
  goal: >
    Write up the Charlie Munger-style investment signal as a section to be added to a comprehensive stock research report.
  backstory: >
    You are an award-winning copywriter known for your clear and engaging style.
    You are collaborating with Charlie Munger, the vice chairman of Berkshire Hathaway, to articulate his investment signal for inclusion in a comprehensive stock research report.
    Your task is to accurately and compellingly convey his investment philosophy, which centers on:
    1.  Quality and Predictability: Prioritizing businesses with understandable and reliable operations.
    2.  Mental Models: Utilizing a multidisciplinary framework of mental models for thorough analysis.
    3.  Durable Competitive Advantages (Moats): Seeking companies with strong, lasting competitive advantages.
    4.  Long-Term Thinking: Emphasizing patience and a long-term investment horizon.
    5.  Management Integrity: Valuing the integrity and competence of company leadership.
    6.  High Returns on Invested Capital: Focusing on businesses that generate high returns on invested capital.
    7.  Fair Price: Paying a reasonable price for exceptional businesses.
    8.  Margin of Safety: Always demanding a margin of safety to protect against misjudgment.
    9.  Simplicity: Avoiding complex businesses that are difficult to understand.
    10. Inversion: Focusing on avoiding errors and pitfalls ("avoiding stupidity") rather than striving for extraordinary brilliance.

    Your writing should reflect Munger's emphasis on value, long-term growth, and rational decision-making.

File: 
crews/charlie_munger_crew/config/tasks.yaml
Content: 
charlie_munger_analysis_task:
  description: >
    Conduct a comprehensive analysis of the specified stock and formulate an investment recommendation,
    strictly adhering to the investment principles of Charlie Munger.

    Company name: {company_name}

    Ticker: {company_ticker}

    Key Considerations:
    - Praise businesses with predictable, consistent operations and cash flows.
    - Value businesses with high ROIC and pricing power.
    - Prefer simple businesses with understandable economics.
    - Admire management with skin in the game and shareholder-friendly capital allocation.
    - Focus on long-term economics rather than short-term metrics.
    - Be skeptical of businesses with rapidly changing dynamics or excessive share dilution.
    - Avoid excessive leverage or financial engineering.
    - Provide a rational, data-driven recommendation (bullish, bearish, or neutral).

    Guidelines:
    - Assess the consistency and predictability of operations and cash flows.
    - Evaluate ROIC, pricing power, and the presence of a durable competitive advantage (moat).
    - Analyze the business model for simplicity and understandability.
    - Examine management's track record, incentives, and capital allocation decisions.
    - Prioritize long-term value creation over short-term financial performance.
    - Investigate any potential for rapid industry changes or significant share dilution.
    - Scrutinize the balance sheet for excessive debt or complex financial instruments.

    Leverage the available tools to gather all necessary data.
    Synthesize your findings into a clear, Charlie Munger-style investment signal that substantiates the investment recommendation with detailed reference to specific metrics in the analysis.

  expected_output: >
    A rational recommendation (bullish, bearish, or neutral), with a confidence level (0-100) and concise reasoning.
  agent: charlie_munger_agent

charlie_munger_writeup_task:
  description: >
    Compose a concise, compelling summary of the Charlie Munger-style investment signal. This summary will be
    incorporated as a distinct section within a broader, comprehensive stock research report.

    Company name: {company_name}

    Ticker: {company_ticker}

    Current date: {date_us}

    Formatting and Style Guidelines:
    - Begin with the heading: ### Charlie Munger-style investment analysis
    - Adopt a first-person perspective, writing as if you are Charlie Munger.
    {expert_analyst_writer_prompt}

  expected_output: >
    A succinct, Markdown-formatted summary of the investment signal, free of code blocks, presenting a clear and definitive investment perspective.
  agent: charlie_munger_writer

crews/chart_reading_crew/chart_reading_crew.py
Content: 
from crewai import Agent, Crew, Process, Task
from crewai.project import CrewBase, agent, crew, task

# If you want to run a snippet of code before or after the crew starts, 
# you can use the @before_kickoff and @after_kickoff decorators
# https://docs.crewai.com/concepts/crews#example-crew-class-with-decorators

@CrewBase
class ChartReadingCrew():
	"""ChartReadingCrew crew"""

	# Learn more about YAML configuration files here:
	# Agents: https://docs.crewai.com/concepts/agents#yaml-configuration-recommended
	# Tasks: https://docs.crewai.com/concepts/tasks#yaml-configuration-recommended
	agents_config = 'config/agents.yaml'
	tasks_config = 'config/tasks.yaml'

	# If you would like to add tools to your agents, you can learn more about it here:
	# https://docs.crewai.com/concepts/agents#agent-tools
	@agent
	def researcher(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['researcher'],
			verbose=True
		)

	@agent
	def reporting_analyst(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['reporting_analyst'],
			verbose=True
		)

	# To learn more about structured task outputs, 
	# task dependencies, and task callbacks, check out the documentation:
	# https://docs.crewai.com/concepts/tasks#overview-of-a-task
	@task
	def research_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['research_task'],
		)

	@task
	def reporting_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['reporting_task'],
			output_file='report.md'
		)

	@crew
	def crew(self) -> Crew:
		"""Creates the ChartReadingCrew crew"""
		# To learn how to add knowledge sources to your crew, check out the documentation:
		# https://docs.crewai.com/concepts/knowledge#what-is-knowledge

		return Crew(
			agents=self.agents, # Automatically created by the @agent decorator
			tasks=self.tasks, # Automatically created by the @task decorator
			process=Process.sequential,
			verbose=True,
			# process=Process.hierarchical, # In case you wanna use that instead https://docs.crewai.com/how-to/Hierarchical/
		)

crews/competitor_crew/competitor_crew.py
Content: 
import os
from stock_analyser.utils.models import CompetitorList, CompetitorFinancialData, CompetitorSection
from stock_analyser.utils.constants import TIMESTAMP, REL_KNOW_DIR
from stock_analyser.utils.agent_llms import RESEARCH_MODEL, EXPERT_MODEL
from crewai import Agent, Crew, Process, Task
from crewai.project import CrewBase, agent, crew, task
from stock_analyser.tools.gemini_search_tool import GeminiSearchTool
from stock_analyser.tools.yfinance_competitor_kpis_tool import YFinanceCompetitorKPIsTool
from stock_analyser.tools.trafilatura_webscrape import TrafilaturaWebscrapeTool
from stock_analyser.tools.tavily_search import TavilySearchTool
from stock_analyser.tools.google_search_tool import GoogleSearchTool
from stock_analyser.tools.yfinance_competitor_news_tool import YFinanceCompetitorNewsTool
from stock_analyser.tools.yfinance_industry_leaders_tool import YFinanceIndustryLeadersTool
from dotenv import load_dotenv

load_dotenv()

google_search_tool = GoogleSearchTool(api_key=os.getenv("GOOGLE_SEARCH_API_KEY"), cx=os.getenv("SEARCH_ENGINE_ID"))

	
@CrewBase
class CompetitorCrew():
	"""CompetitorCrew crew"""

	agents_config = 'config/agents.yaml'
	tasks_config = 'config/tasks.yaml'

	# ----Agents----#
	@agent
	def competitor_identifier_agent(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['competitor_identifier_agent'],
			verbose=True,
			tools=[
				GeminiSearchTool(),
				YFinanceIndustryLeadersTool(),
				TavilySearchTool(),
				google_search_tool,
				TrafilaturaWebscrapeTool()
			],
			llm=RESEARCH_MODEL
		)

	@agent
	def competitor_financial_data_agent(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['competitor_financial_data_agent'],
			tools=[
				YFinanceCompetitorKPIsTool(),
				YFinanceCompetitorNewsTool(),
				TrafilaturaWebscrapeTool(),
				google_search_tool,
				# CompetitorNewsSearchTool()
			],
			llm=EXPERT_MODEL,
			verbose=True,
		)
	
	@agent
	def competitor_section_writing_agent(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['competitor_section_writing_agent'],
			llm=EXPERT_MODEL,
			verbose=True,
		)

	# ----Tasks----#
	@task
	def competitor_identification_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['competitor_identification_task'],
			output_pydantic=CompetitorList
		)
	
	@task
	def competitor_financial_data_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['competitor_financial_data_task'],
			output_pydantic=CompetitorFinancialData,
			output_file=f"{REL_KNOW_DIR}/{TIMESTAMP}_competitor_financial_data.md"
		)
	
	@task
	def competitor_section_writing_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['competitor_section_writing_task'],
			output_pydantic=CompetitorSection,
			output_file=f"{REL_KNOW_DIR}/{TIMESTAMP}_competitor_section.md"
		)


	@crew
	def crew(self) -> Crew:
		"""Creates the CompetitorCrew crew"""

		return Crew(
			agents=self.agents, # Automatically created by the @agent decorator
			tasks=self.tasks, # Automatically created by the @task decorator
			process=Process.sequential,
			verbose=True
		)

crews/competitor_crew/config/tasks.yaml
Content: 
competitor_identification_task:
  description: >
    Your task is to research and identify the primary competitors of the following company:

    Company name: {company_name}.

    Company ticker: {company_ticker}.

    Industry: {industry}.

    The competitors you identify will be used to populate a "Competitor Analysis" section of a stock analysis report about {company_name}.

    Use the tools available to you to identify the competitors. These could be leaders in the same industry, or companies that are similar in nature.
    
    Return a list containing just the names and tickers of these competitors.

    Current date (ISO format): {date_iso}.

  expected_output: >
    A `CompetitorList` model instance containing a list of approximately 5 main competitors of {company_name}, including each competitor's name and ticker symbol.
  agent: competitor_identifier_agent

competitor_financial_data_task:
  description: >
    Using the provided list of competitors, gather their financial data using the available tools.
    Compile a comprehensive dataset within the `CompetitorFinancialData` model, including the specified financial metrics for both the competitors and {company_name} ({company_ticker}).

    Current date (ISO format): {date_iso}.

  expected_output: >
    A `CompetitorFinancialData` model instance containing the following data:
    - competitors: A list of competitors, plus {company_name} ({company_ticker}), with the names and tickers of each company.
    - revenue_growth_yoy: Year-over-year revenue growth for {company_name} and each of its competitors.
    - net_margin: Net margin for {company_name} and each of its competitors.
    - return_on_equity: Return on equity for {company_name} and each of its competitors.
    - price_to_earnings_ratio: Price-to-earnings ratio for {company_name} and each of its competitors.
    - price_to_sales_ratio: Price-to-sales ratio for {company_name} and each of its competitors.
    - price_to_book_ratio: Price-to-book ratio for {company_name} and each of its competitors.
    - recent_news_articles: Relevant news articles pertaining to the competitors.
  agent: competitor_financial_data_agent

competitor_section_writing_task:
  description: >
    Review the collected financial data and news articles for {company_name}'s competitors.
    Compose a "Competitor Analysis" section for a stock analysis report that presents this information in a clear, engaging, and easily digestible manner.

    The section should consist of four main sub-sections:
    1. A markdown table summarizing the key financial metrics of the competitors, using {company_name} as a benchmark for comparison.
    2. Analysis of {company_name}'s financial performance relative to its competitors based on these figures.
    3. A placeholder for a chart.
    4. A summary of recent news about the competitors.

    1.) Create a markdown table summarizing the key financial metrics of the competitors, using {company_name} as a benchmark for comparison.
    The table should include:
    - Revenue growth (year-over-year)
    - Net margin
    - Return on equity (ROE)
    - Price-to-sales ratio (PSR)
    - Price-to-earnings ratio (P/E)
    - Price-to-book ratio (P/B)

    2.) Provide analysis of {company_name}'s financial performance relative to its competitors based on these figures.

    3.) After this analysis, precisely insert the placeholder: `[competitorPrices]`.
    Do not add any other text or formatting to this placeholder.
    This placeholder will be replaced later with a chart showing the prices of {company_name} and its competitors.
    Continue with the rest of the section after the placeholder.

    4.) Highlight any significant news or research findings about the competitors that could potentially affect {company_name}.
    Include the source URL where provided, formatted with correct markdown link formatting, e.g. [publicationName](https://example.com)

    You must begin the section `content` with the heading: ## Competitor Analysis

    Do not include a publication date as a separate heading.

    The output should be formatted as markdown (without code blocks) and structured according to the `CompetitorSection` model.
    Make sure that special characters such as '$', '%', and '&' are not escaped.

    Current date: {date_us}.
  expected_output: >
    A `CompetitorSection` model instance containing:
    1) the content for the "Competitor Analysis" section of the report. This must include:
        - a markdown table of the financial data
        - a comparative analysis of {company_name}'s financial performance in comparison to its competitors
        - the placeholder for a chart
        - a summary of any recent relevant news stories about the competitors that might affect {company_name}, including sources formatted with correct markdown link formatting, e.g. [publicationName](https://example.com)
    2) a list of the competitors with their company name and tickers
  agent: competitor_section_writing_agent

from stock_analyser.utils.agent_llms import RESEARCH_MODEL, WRITING_MODEL
from crewai import Agent, Crew, Process, Task
from crewai.project import CrewBase, agent, crew, task
from dotenv import load_dotenv

load_dotenv()


@CrewBase
class ConclusionCrew():
	"""ConclusionCrew crew"""

	agents_config = 'config/agents.yaml'
	tasks_config = 'config/tasks.yaml'



	# ----Agents----#
	@agent
	def planner(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['planner'],
			llm=RESEARCH_MODEL,
			verbose=True
		)

	@agent
	def writer(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['writer'],
			llm=WRITING_MODEL,
			verbose=True
		)

	# ----Tasks----#
	@task
	def planning_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['planning_task'],
		)

	@task
	def writing_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['writing_task'],
		)


	@crew
	def crew(self) -> Crew:
		"""Creates the ConclusionCrew crew"""
		# To learn how to add knowledge sources to your crew, check out the documentation:
		# https://docs.crewai.com/concepts/knowledge#what-is-knowledge

		return Crew(
			agents=self.agents, # Automatically created by the @agent decorator
			tasks=self.tasks, # Automatically created by the @task decorator
			process=Process.sequential,
			verbose=True
		)

planning_task:
  description: >
    Analyze the stock analysis report and comprehensively extract all key information to form the basis of the final "Conclusion" section.

    Report:
    {report}

    Synthesize a holistic summary of the key facts and data contained within the report.
    Make sure the ultimate investment recommendation is clear and emphasised, and provide sufficient justification for why this recommendation has been provided.
    This will subsequently be used to substantify a conclusion on whether there is a compelling investment case for {company_name}, so it is important to include all relevant data.
    Current date (ISO format): {date_iso}.
  expected_output: >
    A concise summary of the report's main points, forming the foundation for the conclusion.
    Presented as bullet points, each a single, impactful sentence.
    Output in markdown, excluding code blocks.
  agent: planner

  description: >
    Synthesize a comprehensive and compelling conclusion for the stock analysis report on {company_name}, drawing directly from the provided context and planner's outline.
    This conclusion must provide a holistic, insightful overview of the entire report, culminating in a definitive, clearly emphasized, and well-justified investment recommendation (Buy, Sell, or Hold).
    The justification should be robust, citing specific evidence and key findings from within the report to support the recommendation. The conclusion should leave no ambiguity about the investment decision.
    Prioritize clarity, conciseness, and persuasive reasoning. Use accessible language, avoiding technical jargon. Focus on the most critical findings of the report that directly inform the investment recommendation, omitting any non-essential details.
    Adhere strictly to the information provided; do not introduce external data or suggest additional research. The conclusion should stand alone as a concise, decisive summary of the investment case.
    Current date: {date_us}.
  expected_output: >
    A complete conclusion that clearly indicates whether the data supports a case for investment in {company_name}.
    Output in markdown formatting, excluding code blocks. Make sure that special characters such as '$', '%', and '&' are not escaped.
    You must begin the section with the heading: ## Conclusion
  agent: writer

File: 
crews/executive_summary_crew/config/tasks.yaml
Content: 
planning_task:
  description: >
    Read the "Stock Analysis Report" and gather and summarize key information for the "Executive Summary" section.

    Report:
    {report}

    Include a high-level company overview (core business, industry, competitive stance),
    recent stock performance highlights (price trend, percentage change, key events),
    major takeaways (e.g., potential growth drivers, risks), and an investment thesis.
    Current date (ISO format): {date_iso}.
  expected_output: >
    A concise outline with bullet points covering: 1. Company Overview, 2. Recent Stock Performance, 3. Major Takeaways, 4. Investment Thesis.
    Each bullet point should be a single, impactful sentence.
  agent: planner

writing_task:
  description: >
    Develop the planner's outline into a compelling, easily digestible narrative for the "Executive Summary" section.
    This summary must effectively communicate the core investment analysis and recommendations to investors.
    Expand each sub-section (Company Overview, Recent Stock Performance, Major Takeaways, Investment Thesis) with concise yet informative detail.
    Guidelines:
    - Begin with a compelling fact, statistic, or statement to emphasize the report's significance.
    - Clearly identify the report's core objective, ensuring the summary remains focused on this central point. Avoid extraneous details.
    - Maintain brevity and conciseness: Aim for a single, well-structured paragraph per sub-section. The executive summary serves as a brief overview, so concision is key.
    - Prioritize the most critical information: Highlight key findings and recommendations pertinent to investors, omitting non-essential details.
    - Employ clear, concise language, avoiding jargon or technical terms unfamiliar to a broad audience. Use straightforward language for maximum comprehension.
    - Conclude with a definitive call to action: End with a clear investment recommendation ("Buy," "Hold," or "Sell") to provide investors with immediate clarity.
    The executive summary is the first part that investors will read; therefore, a strong first impression is crucial.
    Current date: {date_us}.
  expected_output: >
    A complete draft of the Executive Summary.
    Each section should be a well-written paragraph or series of paragraphs, providing a comprehensive overview without unnecessary detail.
    The output must be formatted as markdown without code blocks.
    You must begin the section with the heading: ## Executive Summary
  agent: writer

critic_task:
  description: >
    Perform a rigorous critical review of the draft "Executive Summary" section, focusing on accuracy, clarity, coherence, completeness, and overall impact.
    The executive summary should effectively communicate the investment analysis and recommendations to investors.
     - Identify any factual errors, inconsistencies, or logical gaps.
     - Assess the clarity and effectiveness of the writing. Are concepts explained clearly and succinctly?
     - Evaluate the coherence of the narrative. Does the section flow logically and tell a compelling story?
     - Provide specific, actionable feedback for improvement, referencing line numbers or specific phrases where possible.
     - Ensure the section maintains an objective, data-driven tone and avoids speculation.
     - Do not suggest adding charts, graphs or imagery.
     - You must also output the full, verbatim draft section content that you have critiqued.
    Remember that the executive summary is often the first thing investors will read, so it's important to make a strong first impression.
    Current date (ISO format): {date_iso}.
  expected_output: >
    A detailed critique of the "Executive Summary" draft, including specific suggestions for improvement and
    identification of any factual errors, omissions, or areas needing clarification. Output should be in the `Critique` pydantic model,
    so include the full, verbatim draft content that you have critiqued as well as the critique.
  agent: critic

editing_task:
  description: >
    Refine and finalize the "Executive Summary" section, incorporating the critic's feedback to achieve the highest possible quality.
    Ensure the final version is polished, error-free, and presents a clear, concise, and compelling overview of the company and its stock performance.
    Remember that the executive summary is often the first thing investors will read, so it's important to make a strong first impression.
    Guidelines:
    - Focus on enhancing the content in line with the feedback and avoid cutting content unless it's necessary.
    - Focus on achieving exceptional clarity and accuracy while maintaining conciseness.
    - Use only the available information provided to you; do not introduce new data or suggest further research.
    - Express percentages with two decimal places for precision.
    - Make sure that special characters such as '$', '%', and '&' are not escaped.
    - Avoid unnecessary disclaimers or caveats.
    - Do not explicitly mention the critic's feedback in the revised text.
    - Current date: {date_us}.
    - Do not include the publication date in the section heading or as a separate subheading.
    - Make sure the section begins with the heading: ## Executive Summary
  expected_output: >
    A final, publication-ready "Executive Summary". The summary should be concise,
    accurate, and effectively communicate the key findings of the analysis.
    The output must be formatted as markdown without code blocks.
  agent: editor

crews/executive_summary_crew/executive_summary_crew.py
Content: 
from crewai import Agent, Crew, Process, Task
from crewai.project import CrewBase, agent, crew, task
from stock_analyser.utils.models import ReportCritique
from stock_analyser.utils.constants import TIMESTAMP, REL_KNOW_DIR
from stock_analyser.utils.agent_llms import RESEARCH_MODEL, WRITING_MODEL, CRITIC_MODEL, EDITOR_MODEL

from dotenv import load_dotenv

load_dotenv()


@CrewBase
class ExecutiveSummaryCrew():
	"""ExecutiveSummaryCrew crew"""

	agents_config = 'config/agents.yaml'
	tasks_config = 'config/tasks.yaml'
	

	# ----Agents----#
	@agent
	def planner(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['planner'],
			llm=RESEARCH_MODEL,
			verbose=True
		)

	@agent
	def writer(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['writer'],
			llm=WRITING_MODEL,
			verbose=True
		)

	@agent
	def critic(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['critic'],
			llm=CRITIC_MODEL,
			verbose=True
		)

	@agent
	def editor(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['editor'],
			llm=EDITOR_MODEL,
			verbose=True
		)

	# ----Tasks----#
	@task
	def planning_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['planning_task'],
		)

	@task
	def writing_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['writing_task'],
		)

	@task
	def critic_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['critic_task'],
			output_pydantic=ReportCritique
		)

	@task
	def editing_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['editing_task'],
			output_file=f"{REL_KNOW_DIR}/{TIMESTAMP}_Executive_Summary.md"
		)

	@crew
	def crew(self) -> Crew:
		"""Creates the ExecutiveSummaryCrew crew"""
		

		return Crew(
			agents=self.agents, # Automatically created by the @agent decorator
			tasks=self.tasks, # Automatically created by the @task decorator
			process=Process.sequential,
			verbose=True
		)

comprehensive_financial_analysis_task:
  description: >
    You are researching content for the "Financial Deep Dive" section in a stock analysis
    report about the stock of the following company: 

    Company name: {company_name}.

    Company ticker: {company_ticker}.

    Website: {company_website}

    Industry: {industry}.

    Using the tools available to you and your knowledge sources, gather comprehensive financial data for {company_name} (ticker: {company_ticker}).
    Focus on populating ALL components of the `FinancialData` model:

    1. Income Statement Analysis:
        - Net income, total revenue, gross profit, and operating income.
        - EBITDA, basic EPS, diluted EPS.
        - Gross margins, operating margin, and profit margin.

    2. Balance Sheet Analysis:
        - Total assets, total liabilities, and total equity.
        - Total debt, net debt, cash and equivalents, and retained earnings.
        - Working capital, current ratio, quick ratio, and debt-to-equity ratio.

    3. Cash Flow Analysis:
        - Total cash, free cash flow, operating cash flow, and capital expenditure.
        - Repayment of debt and debt issuance.
        - Cash changes, investing cash flow, and financing cash flow.
        - Repurchases of stock, and depreciation and amortization.

    4. Growth Metrics:
        - Revenue growth
        - Earnings growth
        - 5-year revenue growth rate
        - 2-year revenue growth rate
        - Free Cash Flow growth
    
    5. Valuation Metrics:
        - Market cap.
        - Trailing and forward P/E ratios.
        - Price-to-sales, price-to-book ratios, and book value.
        - Enterprise value, EV/Revenue, and EV/EBITDA ratios.
        - Trailing and forward EPS.

    6. Price Metrics:
        - Current price, 52-week high, 52-week low.
        - 50-day and 200-day moving averages.

    7. Return Metrics:
        - Return on equity, return on assets, and return on invested capital.

    8. Dividend Metrics:
        - Cash dividends paid.
        - Dividend yield, dividend rate, and payout ratio.
        - Ex-dividend date and next dividend date.

    Use all available tools systematically to gather this information.

    Current date (ISO format): {date_iso}.
  expected_output: >
    The complete `FinancialData` Pydantic model with ALL components fully populated:
    
    1. `income_statement_analysis`: Complete `IncomeStatementAnalysis` model with:
        - `net_income`, `total_revenue`, `gross_profit`, `operating_income`,
        - `ebitda`, `basic_eps`, `diluted_eps`
        - `gross_margins`, `operating_margin`, `profit_margin`

    2. `balance_sheet_analysis`: Complete `BalanceSheetAnalysis` model with:
        - `total_assets`, `total_liabilities`, `total_equity`
        - `total_debt`, `net_debt`, `cash_and_equivalents`, `retained_earnings`
        - `working_capital`, `current_ratio`, `quick_ratio`, `debt_to_equity`
    
    3. `cash_flow_analysis`: Complete `CashFlowAnalysis` model with:
        - `total_cash`, `free_cash_flow`, `operating_cash_flow`, `capital_expenditures`
        - `repayment_of_debt`, `debt_issuance`
        - `cash_changes`, `investing_cash_flow`, `financing_cash_flow`
        - `repurchases_of_stock`, `depreciation_and_amortization`

    4. `growth_metrics`: Complete `GrowthMetrics` model with:
        - `revenue_growth`, `earnings_growth`, `five_year_revenue_growth_rate`, `two_year_revenue_growth_rate`, `free_cash_flow_growth`

    5. `valuation_metrics`: Complete `ValuationMetrics` model with:
        - `market_cap`
        - `trailing_pe_ratio`, `forward_pe_ratio`
        - `price_to_sales`, `price_to_book`, `book_value`
        - `enterprise_value`, `enterprise_to_revenue`, `enterprise_to_ebitda`
        - `trailing_eps`, `forward_eps`

    6. `price_metrics`: Complete `PriceMetrics` model with:
        - `current_price`
        - `fifty_two_week_high`, `fifty_two_week_low`
        - `fifty_day_average`, `two_hundred_day_average`

    7. `return_metrics`: Complete `ReturnMetrics` model with:
        - `return_on_equity`, `return_on_assets`, `return_on_invested_capital`

    8. `dividend_metrics`: Complete `DividendMetrics` model with:
        - `cash_dividends_paid`

        - `ex_dividend_date`, `next_dividend_date`

  agent: researcher

import os
from crewai import Agent, Crew, Process, Task
from crewai.project import CrewBase, agent, crew, task
from crewai_tools import QdrantVectorSearchTool
from stock_analyser.utils.embeddings_fn import custom_gemini_embedding_fn
from stock_analyser.utils.models import ReportCritique, FinancialData
from stock_analyser.utils.constants import TIMESTAMP, REL_KNOW_DIR
from stock_analyser.tools.yf_financial_metrics_tool import YFinanceFinancialMetricsTool
from stock_analyser.tools.yfinance_swing_trading_tool import YFinanceSwingTradingTool
from stock_analyser.tools.yfinance_stock_kpi_tool import YFinanceStockKPITool

from stock_analyser.utils.agent_llms import RESEARCH_MODEL, WRITING_MODEL, CRITIC_MODEL, EDITOR_MODEL

from dotenv import load_dotenv

load_dotenv()

qdrant_tool = QdrantVectorSearchTool(
    qdrant_url=os.getenv("QDRANT_CLUSTER_URL"),
    qdrant_api_key=os.getenv("QDRANT_API_KEY"),
    collection_name=os.getenv("QDRANT_COLLECTION_NAME"),
    limit=5,
    score_threshold=0.35,
	custom_embedding_fn=custom_gemini_embedding_fn,
	description = "A tool to search the SEC filings."
)


@CrewBase
class FinancialDataCrew():
	"""FinancialDataCrew crew"""

	agents_config = 'config/agents.yaml'
	tasks_config = 'config/tasks.yaml'


	# ----Agents----#
	@agent
	def researcher(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['researcher'],
			llm=RESEARCH_MODEL,
			tools=[
				YFinanceFinancialMetricsTool(),
				YFinanceSwingTradingTool(),
				YFinanceStockKPITool()
			],
			verbose=True,
			max_iter=10,
			# max_rpm=10
		)

	@agent
	def writer(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['writer'],
			llm=WRITING_MODEL,
			verbose=True
		)

	@agent
	def critic(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['critic'],
			llm=CRITIC_MODEL,
			verbose=True
		)

	@agent
	def editor(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['editor'],
			llm=EDITOR_MODEL,
			verbose=True
		)
	
	# ----Tasks----#
	@task
	def comprehensive_financial_analysis_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['comprehensive_financial_analysis_task'],
			output_pydantic=FinancialData,
			output_file=f"{REL_KNOW_DIR}/{TIMESTAMP}_Financial_Data_complete.md",
		)

	@task
	def writing_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['writing_task'],
		)

	@task
	def critic_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['critic_task'],
			output_pydantic=ReportCritique
		)

	@task
	def editing_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['editing_task'],
			output_file=f"{REL_KNOW_DIR}/{TIMESTAMP}_Financial_Data_section.md"
		)

	@crew
	def crew(self) -> Crew:
		"""Creates the FinancialDataCrew crew"""

		return Crew(
			agents=self.agents, # Automatically created by the @agent decorator
			tasks=self.tasks, # Automatically created by the @task decorator
			process=Process.sequential,
			verbose=True
		)

import os
from crewai import Agent, Crew, Process, Task
from crewai.project import CrewBase, agent, crew, task
from stock_analyser.utils.models import ReportCritique, FutureOutlookModel
from stock_analyser.utils.constants import TIMESTAMP, REL_KNOW_DIR
from stock_analyser.utils.agent_llms import RESEARCH_MODEL, WRITING_MODEL, CRITIC_MODEL, EDITOR_MODEL
from crewai_tools import EXASearchTool
from stock_analyser.tools.gemini_search_tool import GeminiSearchTool
from stock_analyser.tools.qdrant_sec_filings_search_tool import QdrantSECFilingsSearchTool
from stock_analyser.tools.tavily_search import TavilySearchTool
from stock_analyser.tools.trafilatura_webscrape import TrafilaturaWebscrapeTool
from stock_analyser.tools.yfinance_news_tool import YFinanceNewsTool

from dotenv import load_dotenv

load_dotenv()


qdrant_sec_filings_tool = QdrantSECFilingsSearchTool(
	qdrant_url=os.getenv("QDRANT_CLUSTER_URL"),
    qdrant_api_key=os.getenv("QDRANT_API_KEY"),
    collection_name=os.getenv("QDRANT_COLLECTION_NAME")
)

exa_api_key = os.getenv("EXA_API_KEY")
exasearch_tool = EXASearchTool(api_key=exa_api_key, content=True, summary=True)


@CrewBase
class FutureOutlookCrew():
	"""FutureOutlookCrew crew"""

	agents_config = 'config/agents.yaml'
	tasks_config = 'config/tasks.yaml'


	# ----Agents----#
	@agent
	def researcher(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['researcher'],
			llm=RESEARCH_MODEL,
			tools=[
				qdrant_sec_filings_tool,
				GeminiSearchTool(),
				YFinanceNewsTool(),
				exasearch_tool,
				TavilySearchTool(),
				TrafilaturaWebscrapeTool()
			],
			verbose=True,
			max_iter=8,
			# max_rpm=10
		)

	@agent
	def writer(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['writer'],
			llm=WRITING_MODEL,
			verbose=True
		)

	@agent
	def critic(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['critic'],
			llm=CRITIC_MODEL,
			verbose=True
		)

	@agent
	def editor(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['editor'],
			llm=EDITOR_MODEL,
			verbose=True
		)
	
	# ----Tasks----#
	@task
	def research_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['research_task'],
			output_pydantic=FutureOutlookModel,
			output_file=f"{REL_KNOW_DIR}/{TIMESTAMP}_Future_Outlook_research.md"
		)

	@task
	def writing_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['writing_task']
		)

	@task
	def critic_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['critic_task'],
			output_pydantic=ReportCritique
		)

	@task
	def editing_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['editing_task'],
			output_file=f"{REL_KNOW_DIR}/{TIMESTAMP}_Future_Outlook_section.md"
		)

	@crew
	def crew(self) -> Crew:
		"""Creates the FutureOutlookCrew crew"""
	

		return Crew(
			agents=self.agents, # Automatically created by the @agent decorator
			tasks=self.tasks, # Automatically created by the @task decorator
			process=Process.sequential,
			verbose=True
		)

import os
from stock_analyser.utils.agent_llms import EMAIL_MODEL, EDITOR_MODEL
from stock_analyser.utils.models import Email
from crewai import Agent, Crew, Process, Task
from crewai.project import CrewBase, agent, crew, task
from stock_analyser.tools.gmail_tool_with_attachment import GmailAttachmentTool
from dotenv import load_dotenv

load_dotenv()


@CrewBase
class GmailAttachmentCrew():
	"""GmailAttachmentCrew crew"""

	agents_config = 'config/agents.yaml'
	tasks_config = 'config/tasks.yaml'

	@agent
	def email_writing_agent(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['email_writing_agent'],
			llm=EDITOR_MODEL,
			verbose=True
)

	@agent
	def gmail_draft_agent(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['gmail_draft_agent'],
			tools=[GmailAttachmentTool()],
			llm=EMAIL_MODEL,
			verbose=True
)

	@task
	def email_writing_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['email_writing_task'],
			output_pydantic=Email
		)

	@task
	def gmail_draft_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['gmail_draft_task']
		)

	@crew
	def crew(self) -> Crew:
		"""Creates the GmailAttachmentCrew crew"""
		return Crew(
			agents=self.agents, # Automatically created by the @agent decorator
			tasks=self.tasks, # Automatically created by the @task decorator
			process=Process.sequential,
			verbose=True			# process=Process.hierarchical, # In case you wanna use that instead https://docs.crewai.com/how-to/Hierarchical/
		)

Content: 
import os
from stock_analyser.utils.agent_llms import RESEARCH_MODEL, WRITING_MODEL, CRITIC_MODEL, EDITOR_MODEL
from crewai import Agent, Crew, Process, Task
from crewai.project import CrewBase, agent, crew, task
from stock_analyser.tools.gmail_tool import GmailTool
from dotenv import load_dotenv

load_dotenv()


@CrewBase
class GmailCrew():
	"""GmailCrew crew"""

	agents_config = 'config/agents.yaml'
	tasks_config = 'config/tasks.yaml'

	@agent
	def subject_line_writer_agent(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['subject_line_writer_agent'],
			llm=RESEARCH_MODEL,
			verbose=True
)

	@agent
	def gmail_draft_agent(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['gmail_draft_agent'],
			tools=[GmailTool()],
			llm=WRITING_MODEL,
			verbose=True
)

	@task
	def subject_line_writing_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['subject_line_writing_task']
		)

	@task
	def gmail_draft_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['gmail_draft_task']
		)

	@crew
	def crew(self) -> Crew:
		"""Creates the GmailCrew crew"""
		return Crew(
			agents=self.agents, # Automatically created by the @agent decorator
			tasks=self.tasks, # Automatically created by the @task decorator
			process=Process.sequential,
			verbose=True			# process=Process.hierarchical, # In case you wanna use that instead https://docs.crewai.com/how-to/Hierarchical/
		)

Content: 
planning_task:
  description: >
    You are part of a team that is writing an analysis report about the stock of the following company: 
    
    Company name: {company_name}.
    
    Company ticker: {company_ticker}.

    Website: {company_website}

    Industry: {industry}

    Current date (ISO format): {date_iso}.
    
    Your task is to develop a structured set of research questions to supplement the report and support an investment recommendation.

    First read the full report:

    - {market_and_industry_section}
    - {competitor_landscape_section}  
    - {financial_data_section}
    - {analyst_insights_section}
    - {warren_buffet_section}
    - {cathie_wood_section}
    - {benjamin_graham_section}
    - {risk_analysis_section}
    - {future_outlook_section}

    Then, formulate prioritized research questions to guide the investment decision. Consider these areas:
    - Valuation: How do the company's valuation metrics (P/E, P/S, P/B, Revenue Growth, Net Margin, ROE, PSR) compare to those of its competitors?
    - Fundamentals: What are the company's core strengths and weaknesses (e.g., competitive advantages, management quality)?
    - Market Outlook: What are the prevailing market trends and outlook for the company's industry, and how might they affect the company?
    - Catalysts: What potential catalysts (e.g., product launches, regulatory changes) could significantly impact the stock price?
    - Technical Analysis: What do the stock's technical indicators, support/resistance levels, and chart patterns suggest about potential entry/exit points?
    - Additional Considerations: Are there any other pertinent factors to consider, such as a staged buying approach or specific risk mitigation strategies?

    The research questions should be specific, actionable, and relevant to the report's contents.
    Don't ask about information that is already present in the report.
    
  expected_output: >
    A structured research plan with prioritized research questions to supplement the report and inform the investment recommendation.
    Output in a `ResearchQuestions` Pydantic model.
  agent: planner

research_task:
  description: >
    You are researching content for the "Investment Recommendation" section of an analysis report about the stock of the following company: 

    Company name: {company_name}.

    Company ticker: {company_ticker}.

    Website: {company_website}

    Industry: {industry}

    Current date (ISO format): {date_iso}.

    Refer to the content of the report and use the tools you have available to you to gather information in answer to the research questions.

    Provide the answers to the research questions in the `ResearchAnswers` Pydantic model.
    
  expected_output: >
    A structured set of self-contained answers to each of the research questions, crafted for contextual independence.
    Output in the `ResearchAnswers` Pydantic model.
  agent: researcher

writing_task:
  description: >
    Craft a compelling and easily understandable narrative for the "Investment Recommendation" section based on the report's contents and the supplementary research provided to you.
    Clearly and emphatically state a BUY, SELL, or HOLD recommendation, supported by an evidence-based rationale and justified by the research provided.
    While avoiding repetition of existing report content, leverage the report's information to substantiate your recommendation.
    
    Here is the full report to draw on:
    - {market_and_industry_section}
    - {competitor_landscape_section}  
    - {financial_data_section}
    - {trends_section}
    - {analyst_insights_section}
    - {warren_buffet_section}
    - {cathie_wood_section}
    - {stanley_druckenmiller_section}
    - {benjamin_graham_section}
    - {charlie_munger_section}
    - {bill_ackman_section}
    - {risk_analysis_section}
    - {future_outlook_section}

    Guidelines:
    {writer_guidelines}

    - Finish the section with conclusory remarks but do not separate the conclusion from the rest of the section with a sub-section header.


  expected_output: >
    The "Investment Recommendation" section of the report including a clear BUY, SELL, or HOLD recommendation supported by evidence from the report and the supplementary research.
    The output must be formatted as markdown without code blocks.
    You must begin the section with the heading: ## Investment Recommendation
  agent: writer

critic_task:
  description: >
    Perform a rigorous critical review of the draft "Investment Recommendation" section, focusing on accuracy, clarity, coherence, completeness, and overall impact.
    {critic_guidelines}
    - Assessment of the recommendation's clarity and justification.
    Current date (ISO format): {date_iso}.
  expected_output: >
    A detailed critique of the draft, including:
    - Evaluation of the rationale's strength and supporting evidence.
    - Feedback on the relevance and impact of identified catalysts.
    - Review of technical considerations (if any) for accuracy and feasibility.
    - Specific suggestions for improvement, focusing on clarity, conciseness, and data consistency.
    - The full, verbatim draft section content that you have critiqued.
  agent: critic

editing_task:
  description: >
    Refine and finalize the "Investment Recommendation" section, incorporating the critic's feedback to strengthen the justification, clarity, and conciseness of the rationale to achieve the highest possible quality.
    Ensure all supporting information is accurate and consistent, enhancing rather than reducing the existing content.
    Guidelines:
    {editor_guidelines}
    - Current date: {date_us}.
    - Make sure the section begins with the heading: ## Investment Recommendation
    - Do not include an explicit conclusion sub-section header, but do finish the section with conclusory remarks.
    
  expected_output: >
    A final, polished version of the "Investment Recommendation" section, ready for inclusion in the report.
    This should be a fully revised and improved version, addressing all feedback from the critic.
    The output must be formatted as markdown without code blocks.
  agent: editor

crews/investment_recommendation_crew/investment_recommendation_crew.py
Content: 
from crewai import Agent, Crew, Process, Task
from crewai.project import CrewBase, agent, crew, task
from stock_analyser.utils.models import ReportCritique, ResearchQuestions, ResearchAnswers
from stock_analyser.utils.constants import TIMESTAMP, REL_KNOW_DIR
from stock_analyser.utils.agent_llms import RESEARCH_MODEL, WRITING_MODEL, CRITIC_MODEL, EDITOR_MODEL, EXPERT_MODEL
from stock_analyser.tools.gemini_search_tool import GeminiSearchTool
from stock_analyser.tools.yfinance_swing_trading_tool import YFinanceSwingTradingTool

from dotenv import load_dotenv

load_dotenv()


@CrewBase
class InvestmentRecommendationCrew():
	"""InvestmentRecommendationCrew crew"""

	agents_config = 'config/agents.yaml'
	tasks_config = 'config/tasks.yaml'


	# ----Agents----#
	@agent
	def planner(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['planner'],
			llm=EXPERT_MODEL,
			verbose=True
		)

	@agent
	def researcher(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['researcher'],
			llm=RESEARCH_MODEL,
			verbose=True,
			tools=[
				GeminiSearchTool(),
				YFinanceSwingTradingTool()
			],
			max_iter=10
		)

	@agent
	def writer(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['writer'],
			llm=WRITING_MODEL,
			verbose=True
		)

	@agent
	def critic(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['critic'],
			llm=CRITIC_MODEL,
			verbose=True
		)

	@agent
	def editor(self) -> Agent:
		return Agent(
			config=self.agents_config['editor'],
			llm=EDITOR_MODEL,
			verbose=True
		)

	# ----Tasks----#
	@task
	def planning_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['planning_task'],
			output_pydantic=ResearchQuestions,
		)
	@task
	def research_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['research_task'],
			output_pydantic=ResearchAnswers,
		)

	@task
	def writing_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['writing_task'],
		)

	@task
	def critic_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['critic_task'],
			output_pydantic=ReportCritique
		)

	@task
	def editing_task(self) -> Task:
		return Task(
			config=self.tasks_config['editing_task'],
			output_file=f"{REL_KNOW_DIR}/{TIMESTAMP}_Investment_Recommendation_section.md"
		)

	@crew
	def crew(self) -> Crew:
		"""Creates the InvestmentRecommendationCrew crew"""


		return Crew(
			agents=self.agents, # Automatically created by the @agent decorator
			tasks=self.tasks, # Automatically created by the @task decorator
			process=Process.sequential,
			verbose=True
		)
